    mockApi = MockApi();
    when(() => mockApi.getUser(any())).thenAnswer((_) async => User(id

### Question
How do I write widget tests in Flutter?

### Answer
**Widget tests** in Flutter let you test individual widgets and their interactions in isolation. They run in a headless environment and are much faster than integration tests.

### 1. Basic Setup

Widget tests are written using the `flutter_test` package (included by default). Create test files in the `test/` directory with the `_test.dart` suffix.

### 2. Simple Widget Test Example

Here's a complete, working example testing a counter widget:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';

// Your widget under test
class CounterWidget extends StatefulWidget {
  const CounterWidget({super.key});

  @override
  State<CounterWidget> createState() => _CounterWidgetState();
}

class _CounterWidgetState extends State<CounterWidget> {
  int _count = 0;

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Column(
      children: [
        Text('Count: $_count'),
        ElevatedButton(
          onPressed: () => setState(() => _count++),
          child: const Text('Increment'),
        ),
      ],
    );
  }
}

// ==================== TEST ====================

void main() {
  testWidgets('Counter increments when button is tapped', (tester) async {
    // 1. Pump the widget
    await tester.pumpWidget(
      const MaterialApp(home: CounterWidget()),
    );

    // 2. Verify initial state
    expect(find.text('Count: 0'), findsOneWidget);
    expect(find.text('Count: 1'), findsNothing);

    // 3. Simulate user interaction
    await tester.tap(find.text('Increment'));
    await tester.pump(); // Rebuild after state change

    // 4. Verify updated state
    expect(find.text('Count: 0'), findsNothing);
    expect(find.text('Count: 1'), findsOneWidget);
  });
}
```

### 3. Common Finder Patterns

```dart
// By text
find.text('Submit');

// By widget type
find.byType(ElevatedButton);
find.byType(TextField);

// By key (recommended for complex UIs)
find.byKey(const Key('submit_button'));

// By icon
find.byIcon(Icons.add);

// By widget predicate
find.byWidgetPredicate((widget) => widget is Text && widget.data == 'Hello');
```

### 4. Common Actions

```dart
await tester.tap(find.byType(ElevatedButton));
await tester.pump();

await tester.enterText(find.byType(TextField), 'Hello');
await tester.pump();

await tester.drag(find.byType(ListView), const Offset(0, -300));
await tester.pumpAndSettle(); // Wait for animations to finish
```

### 5. Useful Matchers

```dart
findsOneWidget
findsNothing
findsWidgets          // At least one
findsNWidgets(3)
findsAtLeastNWidgets

### Question
How do I write integration tests in Flutter?

### Answer
**Integration tests** in Flutter test the complete app (or large parts of it) running on a real device or emulator. They are the closest thing to end-to-end tests in Flutter.

### 1. Setup

Add the `integration_test` package (it comes from the Flutter SDK):

```yaml
# pubspec.yaml
dev_dependencies:
  integration_test:
    sdk: flutter
  flutter_test:
    sdk: flutter
```

Create the test directory:

```bash
mkdir integration_test
```

### 2. Basic Integration Test Example

**File:** `integration_test/app_test.dart`

```dart
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';
import 'package:integration_test/integration_test.dart';
import 'package:your_app/main.dart' as app;

void main() {
  IntegrationTestWidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();

  testWidgets('Counter increments when FAB is tapped', (tester) async {
    // Launch the app
    app.main();
    await tester.pumpAndSettle();

    // Verify initial state
    expect(find.text('0'), findsOneWidget);
    expect(find.text('1'), findsNothing);

    // Perform action
    await tester.tap(find.byIcon(Icons.add));
    await tester.pumpAndSettle();

    // Verify result
    expect(find.text('0'), findsNothing);
    expect(find.text('1'), findsOneWidget);
  });

  testWidgets('User can navigate to second screen', (tester) async {
    app.main();
    await tester.pumpAndSettle();

    await tester.tap(find.text('Go to Details'));
    await tester.pumpAndSettle();

    expect(find.text('Details Screen'), findsOneWidget);
  });
}
```

### 3. Running Integration Tests

#### On a physical device or emulator:

```bash
flutter test integration_test/app_test.dart -d <device_id>
```

Example:

```bash
flutter test integration_test/app_test.dart -d emulator-5554
```

#### Run all integration tests:

```bash
flutter test integration_test/
```

#### Using `flutter drive` (alternative for CI)

```bash
flutter drive \
  --driver=test_driver/integration_test.dart \
  --target=integration_test/app_test.dart \
  -d <device_id>
```

### 4. Best Practices & Tips

- Keep integration tests focused on **critical user flows** (login, checkout, navigation, etc.).
- Use `pumpAndSettle()` after actions that trigger animations or async operations.
- For more control, you can use `WidgetTester` methods like `enterText`, `scrollUntilVisible`, etc.
- You can also test with **real backend** or use mocks via dependency injection.

### 5. Advanced Example (with text input)

```dart
testWidgets('Login flow works correctly', (tester) async {
  app.main();
  await tester.pumpAndSettle();

  await tester.enterText(find

### Question
How do I mock dependencies in Flutter tests?

### Answer
Mocking dependencies in Flutter tests is typically done with the **mocktail** package (modern, no code generation required) or **mockito**. I'll show the recommended approach using **mocktail**.

### 1. Add Dependencies

```yaml
# pubspec.yaml
dev_dependencies:
  flutter_test:
    sdk: flutter
  mocktail: ^1.0.0
```

### 2. Example Code to Test

```dart
// api_client.dart
abstract class ApiClient {
  Future<String> fetchUserName(int id);
}

// user_repository.dart
class UserRepository {
  final ApiClient apiClient;

  UserRepository(this.apiClient);

  Future<String> getUserName(int id) async {
    final name = await apiClient.fetchUserName(id);
    return name.trim();
  }
}
```

### 3. Writing the Test with Mocktail

```dart
import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';
import 'package:mocktail/mocktail.dart';

// Mocks
class MockApiClient extends Mock implements ApiClient {}

void main() {
  late MockApiClient mockApiClient;
  late UserRepository repository;

  setUp(() {
    mockApiClient = MockApiClient();
    repository = UserRepository(mockApiClient);
  });

  group('UserRepository', () {
    test('returns trimmed username when API call succeeds', () async {
      // Arrange (Stub)
      when(() => mockApiClient.fetchUserName(42))
          .thenAnswer((_) async => '  John Doe  ');

      // Act
      final result = await repository.getUserName(42);

      // Assert
      expect(result, 'John Doe');
      verify(() => mockApiClient.fetchUserName(42)).called(1);
    });

    test('throws exception when API fails', () async {
      when(() => mockApiClient.fetchUserName(any()))
          .thenThrow(Exception('Network error'));

      expect(
        () => repository.getUserName(1),
        throwsA(isA<Exception>()),
      );
    });
  });
}
```

### Key Mocktail Patterns

| Action              | Syntax                                              | Purpose                     |
|---------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|
| **Stub**            | `when(() => mock.method()).thenReturn(...)`         | Define return value         |
| **Async stub**      | `thenAnswer((_) async => value)`                    | Return `Future`             |
| **Any argument**    | `any()` or `any<int>()`                             | Match any argument          |
| **Verify calls**    | `verify(() => mock.method()).called(1)`             | Check how many times called |
| **Never called**    | `verifyNever(() => mock.method())`                  | Ensure method wasn't called |
| **Capture args**    | `verify(() => mock.method(captureAny())).captured`  | Inspect passed arguments    |

### Bonus: Mocking with Arguments

### Question
How do I use Mockito in Flutter?

### Answer
To use Mockito in Flutter, you typically use code generation to create mocks. Here's a complete setup and usage guide:

## 1. Dependencies

Add to `pubspec.yaml`:

```yaml
dev_dependencies:
  flutter_test:
    sdk: flutter
  mockito: ^5.4.4
  build_runner: ^2.4.8
```

## 2. Create a Class to Mock

```dart
// lib/services/user_repository.dart
abstract class UserRepository {
  Future<User> fetchUser(String id);
  Future<void> saveUser(User user);
}

class User {
  final String id;
  final String name;
  User(this.id, this.name);
}
```

## 3. Generate Mocks

Create a test file that annotates which classes to mock:

```dart
// test/user_repository_test.mocks.dart will be generated
import 'package:mockito/annotations.dart';
import 'package:your_package/services/user_repository.dart';

@GenerateMocks([UserRepository])
void main() {}
```

Run the generator:

```bash
flutter pub run build_runner build
```

## 4. Basic Usage

```dart
import 'package:flutter_test/flutter_test.dart';
import 'package:mockito/mockito.dart';
import 'user_repository_test.mocks.dart'; // Generated file
import 'package:your_package/services/user_repository.dart';

void main() {
  late MockUserRepository mockRepository;

  setUp(() {
    mockRepository = MockUserRepository();
  });

  test('fetches user successfully', () async {
    // Arrange (Stub)
    final expectedUser = User('123', 'John');
    when(mockRepository.fetchUser('123'))
        .thenAnswer((_) async => expectedUser);

    // Act
    final result = await mockRepository.fetchUser('123');

    // Assert
    expect(result.name, 'John');
    verify(mockRepository.fetchUser('123')).called(1);
    verifyNever(mockRepository.saveUser(any));
  });

  test('throws exception on error', () async {
    when(mockRepository.fetchUser(any))
        .thenThrow(Exception('Network error'));

    expect(
      () => mockRepository.fetchUser('123'),
      throwsException,
    );
  });
}
```

## 5. Widget Testing with Mocks

```dart
testWidgets('displays user name', (tester) async {
  final mockRepo = MockUserRepository();
  when(mockRepo.fetchUser('1'))
      .thenAnswer((_) async => User('1', 'Alice'));

  await tester.pumpWidget(
    MaterialApp(
      home: UserProfile(repository: mockRepo), // Inject mock
    ),
  );

  expect(find.text('Alice'), findsOneWidget);
});
```

## Key Methods

| Method | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `when().thenReturn()` | Stub synchronous return |
| `when().thenAnswer()` | Stub async/Future return |
| `when

### Question
How do I optimize Flutter app performance?

### Answer
**Flutter App Performance Optimization Guide**

Here are the most impactful, production-proven techniques to optimize Flutter performance, with working code examples.

### 1. Prevent Unnecessary Widget Rebuilds

Use `const` constructors aggressively and `const` widgets.

```dart
// Bad
class MyWidget extends StatelessWidget {
  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Container(
      color: Colors.blue,
      child: Text('Hello'), // Rebuilds every time
    );
  }
}

// Good
class MyWidget extends StatelessWidget {
  const MyWidget({super.key}); // Add const

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return const ColoredBox(
      color: Colors.blue,
      child: Text('Hello'), // const Text
    );
  }
}
```

**Tip**: Use `const` on `Padding`, `SizedBox`, `Icon`, `Text`, etc.

### 2. Use Efficient List Rendering

Always use `ListView.builder` (or `GridView.builder`) for large lists.

```dart
ListView.builder(
  itemCount: items.length,
  itemBuilder: (context, index) {
    return ListTile(
      key: ValueKey(items[index].id), // Important for performance
      title: Text(items[index].name),
    );
  },
)
```

For very complex list items, wrap them in `RepaintBoundary`:

```dart
RepaintBoundary(
  child: ComplexListItem(item: items[index]),
)
```

### 3. Offload Heavy Computation (Isolates)

Never block the UI thread.

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

// Heavy task
int _heavyComputation(List<int> data) {
  return data.fold(0, (a, b) => a + b * 2); // Expensive operation
}

// Usage
Future<int> calculateSum(List<int> data) async {
  return await compute(_heavyComputation, data);
}
```

### 4. Image Optimization

Use `cached_network_image` and proper sizing:

```dart
CachedNetworkImage(
  imageUrl: url,
  memCacheWidth: 300,        // Resize in memory
  memCacheHeight: 300,
  placeholder: (context, url) => const CircularProgressIndicator(),
  errorWidget: (context, url, error) => const Icon(Icons.error),
)
```

Also prefer `AssetImage` with proper resolution-aware assets.

### 5. State Management Best Practices

- Prefer `ValueNotifier` + `ValueListenableBuilder` for simple state.
- Use `Selector` in Provider or `BlocSelector` to rebuild only when needed.

Example with Provider:

```dart
Consumer<UserModel>(
  builder: (context, user, child) {
    return Text(user.name); // Only rebuilds when name changes
  },
)
```

### 6. Use DevTools to Find Bottlenecks

Run this

### Question
What is const constructor in Flutter?

### Answer
A **const constructor** in Flutter (Dart) allows you to create **compile-time constant** objects. These objects are immutable and can be reused by the Dart runtime instead of creating new instances every time.

### Why It Matters in Flutter

Flutter’s widget tree is rebuilt frequently. Using `const` helps Flutter:
- Avoid unnecessary widget recreation
- Reduce memory allocations
- Improve performance (especially in lists or complex UIs)

### Requirements for a `const` Constructor

To mark a constructor as `const`, the class must follow these rules:

- All instance variables must be `final`
- The constructor body must be empty (or only contain `assert` statements)
- It cannot have any non-final fields or run any logic

### Example: Creating a `const` Widget

```dart
class CustomText extends StatelessWidget {
  final String text;
  final Color color;

  // const constructor
  const CustomText({
    super.key,
    required this.text,
    this.color = Colors.black,
  });

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Text(
      text,
      style: TextStyle(color: color, fontSize: 18),
    );
  }
}
```

### Usage Comparison

```dart
// Without const - new instance created on every build
CustomText(text: 'Hello')

// With const - same instance reused (better performance)
const CustomText(text: 'Hello')
```

### Real-World Example in a Widget Tree

```dart
class MyScreen extends StatelessWidget {
  const MyScreen({super.key});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return const Column(
      children: [
        CustomText(text: 'Title'),
        SizedBox(height: 20),           // const
        CustomText(text: 'Subtitle'),
      ],
    );
  }
}
```

### Important Notes

- You can only use `const` when all constructor arguments are also compile-time constants.
- `const` collections are also possible:
  ```dart
  const colors = [Colors.red, Colors.blue];
  ```
- Using `const` on a widget tells Flutter: *"This widget will never change"*, so it can safely skip rebuilding it.

**Rule of thumb**: Use `const` wherever possible — especially with `Text`, `SizedBox`, `Padding`, `Icon`, and your own stateless widgets.

### Question
How do I use RepaintBoundary in Flutter?

### Answer
`RepaintBoundary` creates a separate compositing layer for its child, preventing it from repainting when ancestor widgets repaint. This is crucial for performance when you have expensive widgets (like `CustomPaint`, complex layouts, or images) inside frequently-updating parents (animations, scrolling, setState calls).
## Basic Usage
Wrap the widget that should not repaint:
```dart
RepaintBoundary(
 child: ExpensiveChart(data: staticData),
)
```
## Practical Example
Here's a common scenario: an animated widget causing an expensive static child to repaint every frame.
```dart
class PerformanceExample extends StatefulWidget {
 @override
 State<PerformanceExample> createState() => _PerformanceExampleState();
}
class _PerformanceExampleState extends State<PerformanceExample>
 with SingleTickerProviderStateMixin {
 late final AnimationController _controller;
 @override
 void initState() {
 super.initState();
 _controller = AnimationController(
 duration: const Duration(seconds: 2),
 vsync: this,
 )..repeat();
 }
 @override
 Widget build(BuildContext context) {
 return Column(
 children: [
 // Animated widget repaints every frame
 RotationTransition(
 turns: _controller,
 child: const FlutterLogo(size: 100),
 ),
 const SizedBox(height: 50),
 // Without RepaintBoundary, this repaints every frame
 // With it, it only paints once and stays in its own layer
 RepaintBoundary(
 child: CustomPaint(
 size: const Size(300, 200),
 painter: ExpensiveChartPainter(),
 ),
 ),
 ],
 );
 }
 @override
 void dispose() {
 _controller.dispose();
 super.dispose();
 }
}
class ExpensiveChartPainter extends CustomPainter {
 @override
 void paint(Canvas canvas, Size size) {
 // Expensive drawing operations
 final paint = Paint()..color = Colors.blue;
 for (int i = 0; i < 1000; i++) {
 canvas.drawCircle(
 Offset(
 size.width * (i % 100) / 100,
 size.height * (i / 100) / 10,
 ),
 2,
 paint,
 );
 }
 }
 @override
 bool shouldRepaint(covariant CustomPainter oldDelegate) => false;
}
```
## Screenshot/Export Use Case
`RepaintBoundary` is also required for capturing widget screenshots:
```dart
final GlobalKey boundaryKey = GlobalKey();
RepaintBoundary(
 key: boundaryKey,
 child: YourWidget(),
)
// Later, capture the image:
RenderRepaintBoundary boundary = boundaryKey.currentContext!.findRenderObject() as RenderRepaintBoundary;
ui.Image image = await boundary.toImage(pixelRatio: 3.0);
```
## When to Use
- **Use when**: The child is expensive to paint and doesn't need to update when the parent does
- **Avoid when**: The widget needs to update frequently anyway, or when the overhead of an extra layer isn't worth the savings
- **Check with**: Flutter DevTools → Performance tab → look for "rep

### Question
How do I reduce rebuilds in Flutter?

### Answer
To reduce unnecessary widget rebuilds in Flutter, focus on minimizing the scope of rebuilds, leveraging `const`, and using granular state updates.

Here are the most effective techniques with examples:

### 1. Extract Widgets + Use `const`

The most impactful change is splitting large widgets and marking static parts as `const`.

**Before (rebuilds everything):**
```dart
class MyScreen extends StatelessWidget {
  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Column(
      children: [
        Text('Header'),           // rebuilds unnecessarily
        ExpensiveWidget(),        // rebuilds unnecessarily
        Text(state.value),        // only this needs to rebuild
      ],
    );
  }
}
```

**After:**
```dart
class MyScreen extends StatelessWidget {
  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return const Column(
      children: [
        HeaderWidget(),           // const = no rebuild
        ExpensiveWidget(),        // const = no rebuild
        DynamicSection(),         // only this rebuilds
      ],
    );
  }
}

class HeaderWidget extends StatelessWidget {
  const HeaderWidget({super.key}); // Important

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) => const Text('Header');
}
```

### 2. Use `Selector` / `Consumer` (Provider)

Only rebuild when specific data changes:

```dart
Selector<MyModel, String>(
  selector: (_, model) => model.userName,
  builder: (context, name, child) {
    return Text(name); // Only rebuilds when name changes
  },
)
```

With `child` for static parts:
```dart
Selector<MyModel, int>(
  selector: (_, model) => model.count,
  builder: (context, count, child) {
    return Column(
      children: [
        Text('$count'),
        child!, // Never rebuilds
      ],
    );
  },
  child: const ExpensiveStaticWidget(),
)
```

### 3. Use Riverpod's `select`

```dart
final name = ref.watch(userProvider.select((u) => u.name));
```

### 4. Use `ValueListenableBuilder` / `AnimatedBuilder`

For simple state changes without full widget tree involvement:

```dart
final ValueNotifier<int> counter = ValueNotifier(0);

ValueListenableBuilder<int>(
  valueListenable: counter,
  builder: (context, value, child) {
    return Text('$value');
  },
)
```

### 5. Keys + `shouldRebuild` (Advanced)

For custom control:

```dart
class MyWidget extends StatelessWidget {
  const MyWidget({super.key});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return const _StaticChild(key: ValueKey('static'));
  }
}

class _StaticChild extends StatelessWidget {
  const _StaticChild({super.key});

  @override
  bool operator ==(Object other) => identical

### Question
What is the difference between keys in Flutter?

### Answer
In Flutter, **Keys** are identifiers for widgets that help the framework preserve state when widgets move within the tree or when their position changes among siblings. Without keys, Flutter may reuse the wrong `State` object, causing UI bugs.

## LocalKey vs GlobalKey

| Feature | `LocalKey` | `GlobalKey` |
|---------|------------|-------------|
| **Scope** | Same parent | Entire app |
| **Performance** | Lightweight | Expensive |
| **Access State** | No | Yes (`currentState`) |
| **Access Widget** | No | Yes (`currentWidget`) |
| **Access Context** | No | Yes (`currentContext`) |

---

## LocalKey Subclasses

### 1. **ValueKey** (Most Common)
Use when you have a unique, stable value (like an ID from a database).

```dart
ListView.builder(
  itemCount: items.length,
  itemBuilder: (context, index) {
    return ListTile(
      key: ValueKey(items[index].id), // Stable ID
      title: Text(items[index].name),
    );
  },
)
```

### 2. **ObjectKey**
Use when the identity of an object itself matters (not a property).

```dart
// Two objects with same properties but different instances
final person1 = Person(name: 'John');
final person2 = Person(name: 'John');

Widget build(BuildContext context) {
  return Column(
    children: [
      MyWidget(key: ObjectKey(person1), person: person1),
      MyWidget(key: ObjectKey(person2), person: person2),
    ],
  );
}
```

### 3. **UniqueKey**
Generates a unique key each time. Use when you want to force a widget to be recreated (losing its state).

```dart
// Force a widget to reset its state
MyForm(key: UniqueKey())
```

---

## GlobalKey

Use when you need to access a widget's state, context, or widget from anywhere.

```dart
class _MyHomePageState extends State<MyHomePage> {
  final GlobalKey<FormState> _formKey = GlobalKey<FormState>();

  void _submit() {
    if (_formKey.currentState!.validate()) {
      _formKey.currentState!.save();
    }
  }

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Form(
      key: _formKey,
      child: Column(
        children: [
          TextFormField(validator: (v) => v!.isEmpty ? 'Required' : null),
          ElevatedButton(onPressed: _submit, child: Text('Submit')),
        ],
      ),
    );
  }
}
```

---

## Practical Example: Preserving State

```dart
class ColorBox extends StatefulWidget {
  final Color color;
  const ColorBox({Key? key, required this.color}) : super(key: key);

  @override


### Question
How do I implement push notifications in Flutter?

### Answer
**Implementing Push Notifications in Flutter (Recommended Approach)**

The most reliable way to implement push notifications in Flutter is using **Firebase Cloud Messaging (FCM)** with the `firebase_messaging` package.

### 1. Add Dependencies

```yaml
dependencies:
  firebase_core: ^2.24.2
  firebase_messaging: ^14.7.10
  flutter_local_notifications: ^17.1.2   # For showing notifications in foreground
```

### 2. Firebase Project Setup

- Create a Firebase project at [console.firebase.google.com](https://console.firebase.google.com)
- Add Android and iOS apps
- Download `google-services.json` (Android) and `GoogleService-Info.plist` (iOS)
- Place them in the correct folders:
  - Android: `android/app/google-services.json`
  - iOS: `ios/Runner/GoogleService-Info.plist`

### 3. Basic Implementation

#### `main.dart`

```dart
import 'package:firebase_core/firebase_core.dart';
import 'package:firebase_messaging/firebase_messaging.dart';
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'notification_service.dart';

Future<void> _firebaseMessagingBackgroundHandler(RemoteMessage message) async {
  await Firebase.initializeApp();
  print("Handling background message: ${message.messageId}");
}

void main() async {
  WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();
  await Firebase.initializeApp();

  FirebaseMessaging.onBackgroundMessage(_firebaseMessagingBackgroundHandler);

  await NotificationService().initialize();

  runApp(const MyApp());
}
```

#### `notification_service.dart`

```dart
import 'package:firebase_messaging/firebase_messaging.dart';
import 'package:flutter_local_notifications/flutter_local_notifications.dart';

class NotificationService {
  final FirebaseMessaging _messaging = FirebaseMessaging.instance;
  final FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin _localNotifications = 
      FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin();

  Future<void> initialize() async {
    // Request permission (iOS + Android 13+)
    NotificationSettings settings = await _messaging.requestPermission(
      alert: true,
      badge: true,
      sound: true,
    );

    if (settings.authorizationStatus == AuthorizationStatus.authorized) {
      print('User granted permission');
    }

    // Get FCM Token
    String? token = await _messaging.getToken();
    print("FCM Token: $token");

    // Initialize local notifications
    const AndroidInitializationSettings androidSettings =
        AndroidInitializationSettings('@mipmap/ic_launcher');

    const InitializationSettings initSettings = InitializationSettings(
      android: androidSettings,
      iOS: DarwinInitializationSettings(),
    );

    await _localNotifications.initialize(initSettings);

    // Foreground messages
    FirebaseMessaging.onMessage.listen((RemoteMessage message) {
      _showNotification(message);
    });

    // When app is opened from terminated state
    FirebaseMessaging.onMessageOpenedApp.listen((RemoteMessage message) {
      print("App opened from notification: ${message.data

### Question
How do I use platform channels in Flutter?

### Answer
**Platform Channels** allow Dart code to communicate with native platform code (Kotlin/Java on Android, Swift/Objective-C on iOS).

The most common type is **`MethodChannel`**.

### 1. Dart Side (Flutter)

```dart
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';

class BatteryChannel {
  static const MethodChannel _channel = 
      MethodChannel('com.example.battery');

  static Future<int?> getBatteryLevel() async {
    try {
      final int? batteryLevel = 
          await _channel.invokeMethod<int>('getBatteryLevel');
      return batteryLevel;
    } on PlatformException catch (e) {
      print("Failed to get battery level: ${e.message}");
      return null;
    }
  }
}
```

### 2. Android (Kotlin)

Edit `MainActivity.kt`:

```kotlin
package com.example.yourapp

import androidx.annotation.NonNull
import io.flutter.embedding.android.FlutterActivity
import io.flutter.embedding.engine.FlutterEngine
import io.flutter.plugin.common.MethodChannel

class MainActivity : FlutterActivity() {
    private val CHANNEL = "com.example.battery"

    override fun configureFlutterEngine(@NonNull flutterEngine: FlutterEngine) {
        super.configureFlutterEngine(flutterEngine)
        
        MethodChannel(
            flutterEngine.dartExecutor.binaryMessenger, 
            CHANNEL
        ).setMethodCallHandler { call, result ->
            if (call.method == "getBatteryLevel") {
                val batteryLevel = getBatteryLevel()
                if (batteryLevel != -1) {
                    result.success(batteryLevel)
                } else {
                    result.error("UNAVAILABLE", "Battery level not available.", null)
                }
            } else {
                result.notImplemented()
            }
        }
    }

    private fun getBatteryLevel(): Int {
        // Android battery code here...
        return 87 // example
    }
}
```

### 3. iOS (Swift)

Edit `AppDelegate.swift`:

```swift
import Flutter
import UIKit

@UIApplicationMain
@objc class AppDelegate: FlutterAppDelegate {
  override func application(
    _ application: UIApplication,
    didFinishLaunchingWithOptions launchOptions: [UIApplication.LaunchOptionsKey: Any]?
  ) -> Bool {
    let controller : FlutterViewController = window?.rootViewController as! FlutterViewController
    let batteryChannel = FlutterMethodChannel(
        name: "com.example.battery",
        binaryMessenger: controller.binaryMessenger
    )
    
    batteryChannel.setMethodCallHandler { (call: FlutterMethodCall, result: @escaping FlutterResult) in
        if call.method == "getBatteryLevel" {
            result(self.getBatteryLevel())
        } else {
            result(FlutterMethodNotImplemented)
        }
    }

    return super.application(application, didFinishLaunchingWithOptions: launchOptions)
  }

  private func getBatteryLevel() -> Int {
    // iOS battery code here...
    return

### Question
How do I access camera in Flutter?

### Answer
To access the camera in Flutter, use the official `camera` package. It gives you full control over the camera preview, photo capture, and video recording.

### 1. Add Dependencies

```yaml
dependencies:
  camera: ^0.10.5+5
  permission_handler: ^11.3.1   # Recommended for cleaner permission handling
```

Run `flutter pub get`.

### 2. Platform Configuration

**Android** (`android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`):

```xml
<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.CAMERA" />
<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.RECORD_AUDIO" /> <!-- For video -->
```

**iOS** (`ios/Runner/Info.plist`):

```xml
<key>NSCameraUsageDescription</key>
<string>This app needs camera access to take photos and videos.</string>
<key>NSMicrophoneUsageDescription</key>
<string>This app needs microphone access for video recording.</string>
```

### 3. Working Example

Here's a complete, minimal example:

```dart
import 'package:camera/camera.dart';
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:permission_handler/permission_handler.dart';

class CameraPage extends StatefulWidget {
  const CameraPage({super.key});

  @override

  bool _isCameraInitialized = false;

    super.initState();
    _initializeCamera();
  }

  Future<void> _initializeCamera() async {
    // Request permission
    final status = await Permission.camera.request();
    if (status != PermissionStatus.granted) {
      return;
    }

    // Get available cameras
    _cameras = await availableCameras();
    if (_cameras!.isEmpty) return;

    // Initialize controller (use back camera by default)
    _controller = CameraController(
      _cameras![0],
      ResolutionPreset.high,
      enableAudio: true,
    );

    await _controller!.initialize();

    if (!mounted) return;
    setState(() => _isCameraInitialized = true);
  }

  Future<void> _takePicture() async {
    if (!_controller!.value.isInitialized) return;

    final XFile image = await _controller!.takePicture();
    print('Picture saved at: ${image.path}');
    // TODO: Navigate to preview or process the image
  }

  @override
  void dispose() {
    _controller?.dispose();
    super.dispose();
  }

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Scaffold(
      appBar: AppBar(title: const Text('Camera')),
      body: _isCameraInitialized
          ? CameraPreview(_controller!)
          : const Center

### Question
How do I access location in Flutter?

### Answer
To access device location in Flutter, use the **`geolocator`** package (the most maintained and feature-rich option).

### 1. Add Dependency

```yaml
dependencies:
  geolocator: ^9.0.2
```

### 2. Platform Configuration

**Android** (`android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`):

```xml
<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION" />
<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION" />
```

**iOS** (`ios/Runner/Info.plist`):

```xml
<key>NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription</key>
<string>This app needs access to location when open.</string>
<key>NSLocationAlwaysUsageDescription</key>
<string>This app needs access to location when in the background.</string>
```

### 3. Basic Usage Example

```dart
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:geolocator/geolocator.dart';

class LocationService {
  /// Check and request location permission
  static Future<bool> _handlePermission() async {
    bool serviceEnabled;
    LocationPermission permission;

    serviceEnabled = await Geolocator.isLocationServiceEnabled();
    if (!serviceEnabled) {
      return false;
    }

    permission = await Geolocator.checkPermission();
    if (permission == LocationPermission.denied) {
      permission = await Geolocator.requestPermission();
      if (permission == LocationPermission.denied) {
        return false;
      }
    }

    if (permission == LocationPermission.deniedForever) {
      return false;
    }

    return true;
  }

  /// Get current position
  static Future<Position?> getCurrentLocation() async {
    final hasPermission = await _handlePermission();
    if (!hasPermission) return null;

    return await Geolocator.getCurrentPosition(
      desiredAccuracy: LocationAccuracy.high,
    );
  }
}

// Usage in widget
class LocationExample extends StatefulWidget {
  @override
  State<LocationExample> createState() => _LocationExampleState();
}

class _LocationExampleState extends State<LocationExample> {
  String _location = 'Unknown';

  Future<void> _getLocation() async {
    final position = await LocationService.getCurrentLocation();
    if (position != null) {
      setState(() {
        _location = 'Lat: ${position.latitude}, Lng: ${position.longitude}';
      });
    } else {
      setState(() {
        _location = 'Permission denied or location disabled';
      });
    }
  }

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context) {
    return Scaffold(
      body: Center(
        child: Column(
          mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.center,
          children: [
            Text(_location),
            ElevatedButton(
              onPressed: _getLocation,
              child: const Text('Get Location'),
            ),
          ],
        ),
      ),
    );
  }
}
```

### Question
How do I implement biometric authentication in Flutter?

### Answer
**Implementing Biometric Authentication in Flutter**

The recommended package is **`local_auth`** (maintained by the Flutter team).

### 1. Add Dependency

```yaml
dependencies:
  local_auth: ^2.2.0
```

Run `flutter pub get`.

---

### 2. Platform Configuration

#### **Android**

**`android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`**:

```xml
<manifest xmlns:android="http://schemas.android.com/apk/res/android">
    <uses-permission android:name="android.permission.USE_BIOMETRIC"/>
    <!-- or USE_FINGERPRINT for older devices -->
    
    <application ...>
        ...
    </application>
</manifest>
```

Also ensure your `minSdkVersion` is at least **23** (or 21 with fallback).

#### **iOS**

**`ios/Runner/Info.plist`**:

```xml
<key>NSFaceIDUsageDescription</key>
<string>We need Face ID to authenticate you securely.</string>
```

---

### 3. Implementation

Here's a clean, production-ready implementation:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/material.dart';
import 'package:local_auth/local_auth.dart';
import 'package:local_auth/error_codes.dart' as auth_error;

class BiometricAuthService {
  final LocalAuthentication _auth = LocalAuthentication();

  /// Check if device supports biometric authentication
  Future<bool> canCheckBiometrics() async {
    try {
      return await _auth.canCheckBiometrics && await _auth.isDeviceSupported();
    } catch (_) {
      return false;
    }
  }

  /// Get available biometric types
  Future<List<BiometricType>> getAvailableBiometrics() async {
    try {
      return await _auth.getAvailableBiometrics();
    } catch (_) {
      return [];
    }
  }

  /// Authenticate user
  Future<bool> authenticate({
    String reason = 'Please authenticate to continue',
    bool biometricOnly = true,
  }) async {
    try {
      final bool didAuthenticate = await _auth.authenticate(
        localizedReason: reason,
        options: AuthenticationOptions(
          biometricOnly: biometricOnly,
          stickyAuth: true,           // Keeps auth active across app pauses
          sensitiveTransaction: true,
        ),
      );
      return didAuthenticate;
    } on Exception catch (e) {
      debugPrint('Biometric auth error: $e');
      return false;
    }
  }
}
```

### 4. Usage Example

```dart

  @override

    return Scaffold(

          onPressed: () async {

### Question
How do I use local notifications in Flutter?

### Answer
**Using Local Notifications in Flutter**

The recommended package is **`flutter_local_notifications`**.

### 1. Add Dependency

```yaml
dependencies:
  flutter_local_notifications: ^17.2.1
```

Run:
```bash
flutter pub get
```

### 2. Basic Setup & Initialization

Create a notification helper class:

```dart
import 'package:flutter_local_notifications/flutter_local_notifications.dart';

class NotificationService {
  static final FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin _notificationsPlugin =
      FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin();

  static Future<void> init() async {
    // Android initialization
    const AndroidInitializationSettings initializationSettingsAndroid =
        AndroidInitializationSettings('@mipmap/ic_launcher');

    // iOS/macOS initialization
    final DarwinInitializationSettings initializationSettingsDarwin =
        DarwinInitializationSettings(
      requestAlertPermission: true,
      requestBadgePermission: true,
      requestSoundPermission: true,
    );

    final InitializationSettings initializationSettings = InitializationSettings(
      android: initializationSettingsAndroid,
      iOS: initializationSettingsDarwin,
      macOS: initializationSettingsDarwin,
    );

    await _notificationsPlugin.initialize(
      initializationSettings,
      onDidReceiveNotificationResponse: _onNotificationTapped,
    );

    // Request permission on Android 13+
    await _requestAndroidPermission();
  }

  static Future<void> _requestAndroidPermission() async {
    final AndroidFlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin? androidImplementation =
        _notificationsPlugin.resolvePlatformSpecificImplementation<
            AndroidFlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin>();

    await androidImplementation?.requestNotificationsPermission();
  }

  static void _onNotificationTapped(NotificationResponse response) {
    // Handle notification tap here
    print('Notification tapped: ${response.payload}');
  }
}
```

Call `init()` early in your app (e.g., in `main()`):

```dart
void main() async {
  WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized();
  await NotificationService.init();
  runApp(MyApp());
}
```

### 3. Show a Notification

```dart
Future<void> showNotification({
  required int id,
  required String title,
  required String body,
  String? payload,
}) async {
  const AndroidNotificationDetails androidDetails = AndroidNotificationDetails(
    'channel_id',
    'General Notifications',
    channelDescription: 'This channel is used for general notifications',
    importance: Importance.max,
    priority: Priority.high,
    icon: '@mipmap/ic_launcher',
  );

  const DarwinNotificationDetails iosDetails = DarwinNotificationDetails(
    presentAlert: true,
    presentBadge: true,
    presentSound: true,
  );

  const NotificationDetails notificationDetails = NotificationDetails(
    android: androidDetails,
    iOS: iosDetails,
  );

  await _notificationsPlugin.show(
    id,
    title,
    body,
    notificationDetails,
    payload: payload,
  );
}
```
